1. The champion (score 25910.45) uses: Exploration (state 0) with a transition on perceiving color 3 (blue?) to ZigZag (state 1), and ZigZag with multiple transitions back to Exploration based on neighbor count and gray floor.
2. The floor is BLACK in both strips, GRAY in between. The target is the BLUE-walled strip.

- **State 0**: Exploration or GoToColor(blue) to find the blue strip
- **State 1**: Stop or low-movement behavior when on black floor (target reached)
- Transition: black floor detected → stop/stay

- GoToColor(blue, clr=3) should pull robots toward blue walls

- When on gray floor, explore or go toward blue

Wait, I need to be careful: from state 0 with 3 transitions, D values must go to the other states only. With 2 states, from state 0, only D=0 → state 1.

Let me fix: --n0x2 D=0 → state 1. That's fine, all three go to state 1.

Actually wait: "n0x2 1" means D=1 but with 2 states, from state 0, D can only be 0 (→state1). Let me re-read: "With 2 states, leaving state 0 the only other state is state 1, so D must be 0."

So ALL destinations from state 0 must be D=0 (→state1), and from state 1 must be D=0 (→state0).

FSM: --nstates 2 --s0 8 --vel0 1.0 --cle0 5 --clr0 3 --n0 3 --n0x0 0 --c0x0 0 --p0x0 0.95 --n0x1 0 --c0x1 5 --p0x1 0.005 --n0x2 0 --c0x2 7 --p0x2 0.5 --l0x2 6 --s1 1 --cle1 6 --n1 2 --n1x0 0 --c1x0 1 --p1x0 0.9 --n1x1 0 --c1x1 5 --p1x1 0.001

Wait, but color 6 = cyan. Green walls = color 2? Let me think about colors: the condition C=7 checks for robot-perceived colors. The walls are colored. Blue walls → likely color 3 (blue). Green walls → color 2 (green). 

Actually in AutoMoDe, the color IDs for C=7 are: {1=red, 2=green, 3=blue, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan}. The LED colors use same IDs but with 0=off.

So blue walls = color 3, green walls = color 2.

GoAwayColor from green (clr=2) should move robots away from green walls → toward blue walls. This is

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

The champion (score 12121.02) is a clean 2-state machine:
- **State 0**: Exploration (rwm=20), if perceive blue (color 3) → GoToColor(blue)
- **State 1**: GoToColor(blue, clr=3), if gray floor → back to Explore, if black floor (5%) → stay

This is elegant: robots explore until they see blue walls, then charge toward them. When they reach the black floor (blue strip), they occasionally re-explore (5% chance on black floor), and return to gray → re-explore.

The issue: once on black floor (target), robots have a 5% chance to leave back to exploration (bad!), and a 50% chance on gray floor to go back to exploration. The 0.05 probability on black floor means robots rarely leave, which is good.

**Key insight**: The champion's state 1 transitions back to state 0 (Exploration) when:
1. Gray floor detected (p=0.5) - this means robots in transit keep going
2. Black floor detected (p=0.05) - very small chance to leave target

**Exploitation ideas:**
1. Reduce p on black floor detection (make it even harder to leave target): p=0.02 or 0.01
2. Increase p on gray floor (faster return to GoToColor): p=0.6 or 0.7
3. Adjust rwm (turning rate) for exploration
4. Add a Stop state when on black floor to prevent leaving
5. Try GoAwayColor(green, clr=2) as alternative navigation

**Exploration ideas:**
1. 3-state: Explore → GoToColor(blue) → Stop (when black floor), Stop → Explore (if gray)
2. 2-state: GoToColor(blue) directly, Stop when black floor
3. Use GoAwayColor(green) to push away from wrong strip
4. Add neighbor-count transitions

Let me design 14 machines:

1. **Champion** (exact copy)
2. Exploit: reduce black-floor exit prob to 0.02
3. Exploit: reduce black-floor exit prob to 0.01, increase gray detection to 0.6
4. Exploit: rwm=15 (less random turning), keep structure
5. Exploit: rwm=25, p_blue=0.45
6. Exploit: add black floor → stop transition (3 states)
7. Explore: 3-state with Stop when black floor
8. Explore: GoToColor(blue) primary, Stop secondary
9. Explore: GoAwayColor(green) to find blue strip
10. Explore: 3-state with explicit Stop state on black floor
11. Exploit: from champion with slightly different probs
12. Explore: combine GoToColor(blue) with neighbor awareness
13. Explore: 2-state GoToColor + Stop with multiple transitions
14. Explore: exploration → GoToColor → Stop chain

For the 3-state "add Stop" machine:
- S0: Explore, if see blue → S1
- S1: GoToColor(blue), if black floor → S2 (high prob), if gray → S0
- S2: Stop, if gray → S0, if see blue → S1

## Reasoning

The champion (score 10217.96) is clear:
- **State 0**: Explore (rwm=20), with TWO triggers to GoToColor: (1) perceive blue (p=0.4), (2) perceive green (p=0.4). Both send to state 1.
- **State 1**: GoToColor(blue), returns to Explore on: (1) gray floor (p=0.5), (2) black floor (p=0.02, very rare).

The key insight: triggering on BOTH blue AND green walls helps robots orient faster - seeing either wall helps them navigate toward blue. Once in GoToColor(blue), they mostly stay until they arrive (black floor). The tiny 0.02 probability on black floor keeps robots from getting permanently stuck if they ended up in the wrong (green) strip.

**What to exploit:**
1. Vary p_blue and p_green (the two perception triggers in state 0)
2. Vary rwm (turning rate)
3. Vary gray exit probability (0.5) and black exit probability (0.02)
4. Try adding a 3rd transition from state 0 (e.g., black floor → also go to GoToColor)
5. Try seeing both colors at different probabilities

**What to explore:**
1. 3-state version with Stop when on black floor
2. Add a check: if black floor detected in s0 → also trigger GoToColor (to handle wrong strip)
3. Try different color combinations (e.g., see both blue and green to trigger)
4. Rank-2 was very close (10508): Explore → GoToColor, only black exit (p=0.01). Very clean. Worth fine-tuning.

- Rank 2 (10508): only 1 transition from s1 - on black floor (p=0.01). This means once in GoToColor, robot almost never leaves! Very effective.

- **State 1**: GoToColor(blue, clr=3), only ONE exit: black floor (p=0.01) → back to Explore

The key insight: once a robot sees any colored wall (blue or green), it immediately switches to GoToColor(blue). It stays in GoToColor until it very rarely (1%) gets kicked off by black floor detection. This means robots stay in GoToColor almost permanently once triggered, and GoToColor(blue) drives them toward the blue walls/strip.

The nearly-as-good rank 2 (8572.52) adds a 3rd trigger in state 0: black floor (p=0.5) → also go to GoToColor. This helps robots that start on black floor (wrong strip) to immediately seek blue.

**Exploitation plan:**
1. Champion exact copy
2. Vary p_blue and p_green in s0 (try 0.35, 0.45, 0.5)
3. Vary black floor exit from s1 (try 0.005, 0.015, 0.02)
4. Add black floor trigger in s0 with various probabilities (like rank-2)
5. Try rwm=10 (very slow turning)

**Exploration plan:**
1. Add gray floor trigger in s0 (if gray → GoToColor, makes sense since gray = in transit)
2. Try 3-state version but with Stop rather than return to Explore
3. Try seeing only one color wall but with higher probability
4. Try black floor trigger in s0 with different probabilities

Let me carefully write 14 FSMs. With 2 states: from state 0, D must be 0 (→state 1); from state 1, D must be 0 (→state 0).

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

The champion (score 7856.55) has this structure:
- **State 0**: Explore (rwm=20), THREE triggers all going to GoToColor(blue):
  1. Perceive blue (p=0.4)
  2. Perceive green (p=0.4)  
  3. Gray floor (p=0.5)
- **State 1**: GoToColor(blue, clr=3), ONE exit: black floor (p=0.01) → back to Explore

The gray floor trigger is key: robots in the middle (gray area) immediately switch to GoToColor(blue). Combined with blue and green wall detection, almost any robot that can see or feel its environment will go toward blue.

The rank-2 (8113.25) adds a 4th trigger: black floor (p=0.5) in state 0 → also triggers GoToColor. This helps robots starting on black (wrong strip) to re-orient.

**Exploitation plan:**
1. Champion exact copy
2. Vary gray floor probability (0.4, 0.6, 0.7, 0.8)
3. Vary blue/green perception probability (0.35, 0.5)
4. Vary black floor exit from s1 (0.005, 0.008)
5. Add 4th trigger (black floor) to champion topology
6. Vary rwm (10, 15)

**Exploration plan:**
1. Try gray floor probability very high (0.9) - robots almost always go to GoToColor when on gray
2. Try adding a 4th trigger with different probabilities for black floor
3. Try rwm=5 (very slow turning, nearly straight)

Key observations:
- Gray floor trigger (p=0.5) was the new insight that improved from 8572 to 7856
- Lower rwm seems better (rank-3 had rwm=10 and scored 8271)
- Black exit from s1 at p=0.01 seems optimal (p=0.005 is rank-4, p=0.015 is worst)
- Adding green perception (p=0.4) in s0 is consistently good

Let me write 13 machines carefully, making sure all destination indices are correct. With 2 states, from state I, the only destination D=0 points to the other state.

## Reasoning

The champion (7830.91) uses:
- State 0: Explore (rwm=20), 3 triggers → GoToColor: blue wall (p=0.4), green wall (p=0.4), gray floor (p=0.4)
- State 1: GoToColor(blue), 1 exit: black floor (p=0.01) → Explore

All top performers share the same topology. The gray floor probability is the key variable being tuned:
- p=0.4 → 7830 (champion)
- p=0.8 → 7914
- p=0.5 (prev champion) → 7856 → now 7949
- p=0.7 → 7932
- p=0.35 (blue/green) → 7921

**Observations:**
- rwm=20 seems optimal (rwm=15 was worst)
- p_blue = p_green = 0.4 seems good; p=0.35 also very close
- p_gray in range [0.3, 0.5] seems best (p=0.6 was worst-3, p=0.8 was rank-2)
- black exit p=0.01 seems optimal
- The topology is very converged

**Exploitation:** Fine-tune p_gray (try 0.3, 0.35, 0.45) and p_blue/p_green (try 0.3, 0.38, 0.42)

**Exploration:** 
1. Try 4-transition s0: add black floor trigger (helps robots in wrong strip)
2. Try asymmetric blue/green probabilities
3. Try different rwm values (18, 22)
4. Try p_black_exit = 0.008 in s1
5. Try adding a second trigger to s1 (perceive green → re-explore)

All machines use 2 states. From state 0: all D=0 → state 1. From state 1: all D=0 → state 0.

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

The champion (7830.91) is stable. Key observations from round 7:

- p_blue=p_green=0.3 with p_gray=0.4 scored 7847 (very close to champion)
- p_blue=p_green=0.35 with p_gray=0.4 scored 7917
- rwm=22 scored 7895 (slightly better than rwm=20 in some runs)
- Adding black floor trigger (p=0.7) in s0 scored 7920
- p_black_exit=0.008 scored 7928 (slightly worse than 0.01)
- p_gray=0.3 scored worst-2, p_gray=0.45 scored worst-3

**Key insight:** The champion appears to be near a local optimum. The space of p_blue, p_green (both around 0.3-0.4) and p_gray (around 0.35-0.4) needs finer exploration.

**Exploitation plan:**
1. Champion exact copy
2. Try p_blue=p_green=0.25, p_gray=0.4
3. Try p_blue=p_green=0.3, p_gray=0.35
4. Try p_blue=p_green=0.3, p_gray=0.45
5. Try p_blue=p_green=0.32, p_gray=0.4
6. Try rwm=20, p_blue=0.3, p_green=0.4, p_gray=0.4 (asymmetric)
7. Try rwm=20, p_blue=0.4, p_green=0.3, p_gray=0.4 (asymmetric other way)
8. Try 4 triggers: blue(0.3), green(0.3), gray(0.4), black(0.7)

**Exploration plan:**
1. Try p_black_exit=0.005 with the champion topology
2. Try rwm=24
3. Try rwm=20 with p_gray=0.4 but different blue/green combo
4. Try p_blue=0.2, p_green=0.2, p_gray=0.4 (lower perception thresholds)
5. Try GoToColor with no exit (p=0 effectively impossible - use very low like 0.001)

All machines are 2-state. D=0 is the only valid destination in both directions.

## Reasoning

The champion (7830.91) has been stable for several rounds. This round's best performers all scored ~8000+, which is worse than the champion. The champion's exact parameters seem to be at a local optimum.

**Analysis:**
- Champion: p_blue=0.4, p_green=0.4, p_gray=0.4, p_black_exit=0.01 → 7830.91
- p_black_exit=0.005 → 8012 (slightly worse this round, but was 8421 before - stochastic)
- p_blue=p_green=0.32 → 8033
- Asymmetric p_blue=0.4, p_green=0.3 → 8049
- p_blue=p_green=0.2 → 8152 (bad)
- p_blue=p_green=0.3 → varies: 7847 in round 7, 8169 in round 8 (stochastic)

The scores are very close together and stochastic. The champion at 7830 may be near the true optimum for this topology.

**Strategy for final rounds:**
1. Keep champion
2. Focus exploitation very tightly around champion parameters
3. Try a few genuinely different topologies as exploration (in case there's a better basin)

**Tight exploitation:**
- p_gray: try 0.38, 0.42, 0.36, 0.44
- p_blue=p_green: try 0.38, 0.42, 0.36
- p_black_exit: try 0.007, 0.012
- rwm: try 19, 21

**Exploration:**
- Try 3-state machine with Stop state
- Try GoToColor with no exit at all (extremely low black exit)
- Try different structure altogether

################################################################################
# PROMPT SYSTEME
################################################################################

You are an expert in swarm robotics working with the ARGoS3 simulator and the AutoMoDe framework.
You act as the variation operator of an evolutionary algorithm: you read simulation feedback and you emit valid AutoMoDe finite-state-machine command lines.

# 1. THE MISSION
The score is MINIMISED.

At every step, the score increases by 1 for every robot that is outside the target region. The score does not depend on how close the robots are to each other.

The arena has two strips at opposite ends. The floor is BLACK in both strips and GRAY in between. The target region is the strip whose bordering walls are BLUE; the walls bordering the other strip are GREEN.

# 2. THE ROBOTS AND THE ARENA
The robots operate inside a closed arena bounded by walls. 29 e-puck robots start scattered around the centre of the arena, with random positions and orientations that change at every run. Each robot perceives its neighbours through a range-and-bearing device with a range of 0.32 m: neighbour-count conditions only count robots closer than that. A run lasts 1200 simulation steps (120 s at 10 steps per second).

# 3. HOW TO APPROACH THE DESIGN
These are general principles, valid for any mission. They are not a solution to this one.
- Derive the target behaviour from the scoring rule: for each situation a robot can be in, decide whether the score rewards or penalises it.
- Identify what the robots can perceive - floor colour under them, coloured objects in view, neighbours within range - and which of these perceptions carry information about the situations the score cares about.
- Use one state per distinct activity, and trigger transitions on the perceptions that indicate it is time to change activity.

# 4. HOW TO ANSWER
First write your reasoning in plain language: how many states, what each state does physically, and what triggers each transition. Two or three sentences per machine is enough.
Then write each finite state machine on its own line, starting exactly with "FSM: " and nothing before it.

# 5. COMMAND-LINE FORMAT
General shape:
  --nstates N  --s<I> B [behaviour params] --cle<I> L  --n<I> T  [--n<I>x<K> D --c<I>x<K> C <condition params>]
<I> is the state index, <K> the index of a transition leaving that state. Both start at 0.

## A. Global
* `--nstates N` : number of states, integer in [1..4].

## B. Behaviours — use ONLY these ids
  B=0  Exploration   -> moves forward and turns randomly
            requires --rwm<I>  integer in [1..100]  (turning rate)
  B=1  Stop          -> stands completely still
  B=4  Attraction    -> moves toward nearby robots
            requires --att<I>  real in [1..5]  (attraction gain)
  B=5  Repulsion     -> moves away from nearby robots
            requires --rep<I>  real in [1..5]  (repulsion gain)
  B=6  Spiral        -> traces an expanding spiral
            requires --spd<I>  integer in [1..100]  (spiral speed)
  B=8  GoToColor     -> moves toward the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=9  GoAwayColor   -> moves away from the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=10  FlockCenter   -> moves toward the centre of the group
            requires --fc<I>  integer in [1..100]  (flocking gain)
  B=11  ZigZag        -> moves in a zigzag pattern
            requires --zfr<I>  integer in [1..100]  (zigzag frequency)

Every state also takes an LED colour:
* `--cle<I> L` : one of {0,4,5,6}  (0=off, 1=red, 2=green, 3=blue, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan)

## C. Transitions
* `--n<I> T` : number of transitions leaving state <I>, integer in [1..4].
  Up to 4 transitions may leave the same state, each with a different condition type. A state that reacts to several different situations is often stronger than a state with one transition, and random sampling produces that variety by default.
* `--n<I>x<K> D` : destination, indexed among the OTHER states only.
  With 3 states, leaving state 1 the others are [0, 2]: D=0 targets state 0, D=1 targets state 2.
  With 2 states, leaving state 0 the only other state is state 1, so D must be 0.
* `--c<I>x<K> C` : condition type — use ONLY these ids
  C=0  floor under the robot is BLACK
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=1  floor under the robot is GRAY
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=2  floor under the robot is WHITE
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=3  number of neighbours ABOVE a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=4  number of neighbours BELOW a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=5  fires with a fixed probability at every step
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=7  the robot PERCEIVES a given colour with its camera
            requires --l<I>x<K>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (colour to look for)
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])

# 6. HARD RULES
1. Integers stay integers; real-valued parameters always carry a decimal point (1.0, not 1).
2. Every state that exists must declare `--n<I>`, and every declared transition must carry its destination, its condition type and that condition's parameters. A missing parameter crashes the simulator.
3. `--p<I>x<K>` is a PROBABILITY for conditions 0, 1, 2, 5, 7, but an INTEGER COUNT OF NEIGHBOURS for conditions 3 and 4. Never write 0.8 where a neighbour count is expected.
4. Never point a transition at its own state.

# 7. YOU ARE THE EVOLUTIONARY OPERATOR
This is a multi-round conversation on one real ARGoS3 optimisation run. The numerical optimiser is restarted from scratch at the beginning of every round, so exploration and exploitation are entirely up to you.

1. SURVIVAL (elitism): copy the current best-ever machine verbatim in your answer so its lineage is not lost.
2. EXPLOITATION: spend about half of your machines on small edits of the best performers — shift a probability, a threshold or a gain by roughly ten percent, and keep the topology.
3. EXPLORATION: spend the rest on genuinely different topologies or module combinations, to escape local optima.
4. RICHNESS: do not let every state settle on a single transition of the same condition type. Include candidates with two to four transitions per state mixing different condition types.
